みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 9 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 9 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.54％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では9月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、7月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、8月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

モデルによる9 月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、 図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.54％（年率＋2.17％）と、高い伸びを示す結果になった。他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究
所による9月14日時点のナウキャスティングが同＋0.98％（年率＋3.99％）と、大幅プラスを予測して
いる。一方、日本経済研究センターが公表した9月のESPフォーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月2
日～9月9日、 回答者37名） における民間予測値平均は前期比年率＋0.25％と、 「みずほGDPナ ウ 」の 予
測値対比で小幅な伸びにとどまっている。7～9月期実質GDP予測値については、データドリブン予測
値と民間コンセンサスが大きく乖離していると言えよう。 
両者の乖離は、景気判断の違いというよりも、GDP統計上のテクニカル要因による部分が大きいと
みられる。4～6月期には、中東情勢悪化を背景に原油・ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、石油化学
製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、いずれも
GDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％） とプ
ラス成長になったものの、成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。反
対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の下
方圧力がかかる可能性がある。多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしているのは、こうしたテ
クニカルな要因が考慮されているためだと考えられる。なお、みずほ総合研究所でも、7～9月期GDP
は横ばい圏の伸びにとどまるとみている。 詳細については、 服部直樹 「年率＋1.4％と1次速報から小幅
に上方修正（4～6月期2次QE）」（2026年9月8日） を参照されたい。  
■ 景気実態は改善方向。今後民間予測値が上方修正される可能性にも留意 
データドリブン予測はテクニカル要因を反映しにくい傾向がある反面、より景気実態に近い動きを
捉えているとみられる。 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては、
9月中旬までに公表された月次経済指標を使って予測値を2回更新した。 図表3では、予測値の更新過程
と各月次経済指標の寄与度を示している2。予測値上昇の主因は、8月の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

キャスティング」、日本経済研究センター「ESPフォーキャス
ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 
（注） モデル予測値は、 各時点で知り得るデータを用いて推計した実
質GDP成長率予測値。棒グラフは予測値に対する各経済指標の
寄与度を示す。「みずほGDPナウ」は各経済指標の結果を公表
前に予測しており （モデル予測値） 、 寄与度はモデル予測値と
実績値の乖離幅から計算される。 なお、 各経済指標の確報を受
けて指標が改定された場合も寄与度に反映される

### Page 3

3

通しDI）が高水準となったことである。要因としては、中東危機に起因する供給網混乱の緩和や旺盛
なAI・データセンター関連需要が企業活動を押し上げていることが考えられる。また、鉱工業生産指
数も予測値の押し上げに寄与した。やはり、半導体製造装置などのAI・データセンター関連製品が好
調であるほか、中東情勢悪化の影響で3・4月に落ち込んだ石油化学製品の回復傾向が続いている。 
以上のように、原油やナフサの輸入増、企業の在庫調整といった動きから、GDP統計上の7～9月期
成長率は低い伸びに抑えられるとみている。ただし、「みずほGDPナウ」が示すとおり、足元の景気
実態は4～6月期対比で緩やかな改善傾向にある。総じて企業部門の回復を示す指標が多く、特に鉱工
業生産は8月の生産予測指数（補正値ベース）が前月比＋3.1％となるなど、好調の持続を示唆してい
る。石油化学製品における在庫復元の動きが続く可能性も考慮すれば、民間エコノミストによるGDP
成長率予測値は今後上方修正されるかもしれない。 
次回の「みずほGDPナウ」については、8月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて7～
9月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組むこととし、10月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。中東
情勢悪化やAI・データセンター投資増の影響が入り組む中、ナウキャストによる景気動向の把握はよ
り重要なものとなるだろう。

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

### Page 2

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

1

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
經濟 
⚫ 2026 年第 2 季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值上修至 2.2%， 惟低於第1 季
之 2.5%。成長主要由個人消費及固定投資帶動，其中消費貢獻 2.51 個
百分點；惟進口大增 12.6%，致淨出口拖累 1.10 個百分點。另實質私人
國內最終銷售成長 4.6%，顯示內需仍具韌性。 
⚫ 9 月美國非農就業僅增 2.9 萬人，遠低於前月及近一年平均，失業率升
至 4.2%， 薪資增幅續降 ， 顯示企業聘僱動能轉弱 。 惟勞動市場尚未明顯
惡化，後續仍須關注就業降溫對聯準會利率決策之影響。 
⚫ 8 月美國職位空缺數由 733.5 萬個降至 707.9 萬個，職缺率降至 4.3%，
顯示企業徵才需求略為降溫；惟聘僱人數增至 519.2 萬人。主動離職及
裁員規模同步下降，反映勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整趨於審慎，勞動
市場供需大致維持平衡。 
⚫ 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.5，連續第九個月維持擴張。
新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示需求仍具韌性；惟生產、出
口及進口動能略為放緩，且原物料成本持續攀升，加上關稅政策、地緣
政治及供應鏈不確定性仍存，製造業仍面臨部分壓力。 
⚫ 8 月美國 PCE 物價指數月增 0.3%、年增 3.4%，核心 PCE 月增 0.2%、
年增 3.0%， 顯示通膨大致持穩但短期物價壓力略升 。 另一方面 ， 個人消
費支出月增 0.9%、實質消費成長 0.6%，帶動儲蓄率降至 4.1%，顯示家
庭需求仍具韌性；後續須關注消費熱度及通膨對聯準會政策之影響。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 近期聯準會官員立場趨於分歧，理事 Michael Barr 認為能源價格及 AI
投資熱潮可能延緩通膨降溫 ， 未來仍可能進一步升息 ； 惟John Williams、
Philip Jefferson 及 Michelle Bowman 等官員則主張暫無急於升息的必要 ，
傾向觀察後續經濟數據。加以 9 月非農就業明顯放緩，市場對 10 月升
息預期大幅降溫，焦點轉向 12 月會議。 
⚫ 台積電傳評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作赴德州設廠，雙方仍處討論階
段。合作可能由台積電主導營運，或由 Terafab 持多數股權、台積電提
供技術 。 若成真 ， 將擴大台積電美國布局 ， 並支援Tesla、SpaceX 及 xAI
晶片需求。

2

短評 
⚫ 美國經濟目前仍維持溫和擴張，內需與投資表現優於預期，短期衰退風
險有限 ； 惟近期新增就業明顯低於預期 ， 企業聘僱意願與職缺需求放緩 ，
勞動市場動能已有轉弱跡象，而通膨受能源價格、 AI 投資及關稅等因
素影響仍具黏著性。在經濟成長放緩與通膨尚未完全受控的環境下，聯
準會短期可能傾向維持觀望，惟若通膨再度升溫，不排除年底前仍有進
一步升息空間。 
歐 
元 
區 
法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
經濟 
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨、核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1
個百分點至 3.8%、2.5%。 
⚫ 歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2 個百分點至 14.2%。 
⚫ 歐元區第二季房價年增率為 4%，較第一季降 0.6 個百分點。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大至
125 個基點以上。 
短評 ⚫ 法國財政前景不明朗 ， 未來仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加
劇的風險，暫時不利於消費及投資表現。 
日 
本 
景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%。 
⚫ 8 月零售月減 1.2%。 
⚫ 9 月東京核心通膨年增 2.7%。 
短評 
⚫ 儘管消費動能放緩與通膨壓力升溫 ， 但企業投資與生產回升趨勢仍具韌
性，經濟可望維持溫和復甦，惟日本央行進一步升息的可能性正逐步提
高。 
中 
國 
為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
經濟 ⚫ 9 月官方製造業 PMI、非製造業 PMI 分別上升為 50.1、50.2。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是將資金引導至政
府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍。 
短評 
⚫ 未來若內需復甦力道不如預期 ， 不排除人行將進一步降準或擴大結構性
貨幣工具支持經濟 ； 至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及中美利差等
因素，可能性相對較低。

一、 美國—美國經濟維持溫和擴張，勞動市場降溫與通膨黏著牽動聯準會政策 
2026 年第二季美國實質 GDP 季增年率終值由前次估計之 1.5%上修至 2.2%，
惟仍低於第一季之 2.5%， 顯示經濟持續擴張但成長動能略有放緩 。 此次上修主要
反映投資、消費及政府支出優於原先估計。就成長結構觀察，個人消費支出仍為
主要動能，貢獻 2.51 個百分點，其中服務及商品消費分別貢獻 1.57 及 0.94 個百
分點；私人投資貢獻 0.82 個百分點，以設備投資及智慧財產權投資表現強勁，惟
庫存調整拖累 0.53 個百分點。對外貿易方面，出口成長 5.0%，但進口大幅增加
12.6%， 致淨出口拖累GDP 成長 1.10 個百分點 。 另反映內需基本面的實質私人國
內最終銷售成長 4.6%， 高於第一季之1.8%， 顯示民間需求明顯增強 。 物價方面 ，
第 2 季 PCE 及核心 PCE 物價指數分別上升 5.0%及 3.3%， 顯示通膨壓力仍偏高 。
整體而言，美國經濟仍由消費與固定投資支撐，惟進口快速增加及通膨黏著性，
仍為後續經濟前景的重要觀察重點。 
2026 年 9 月美國非農就業人數增加 2.9 萬人 ， 明顯低於上月修正後的13.3 萬
人，也低於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 4.5 萬人的水準；失業率小幅升至 4.2%，
整體仍維持在 3 月以來 4.1%至 4.3%的區間，反映勞動市場整體變化有限，惟新
增就業力道已轉趨疲弱。家庭調查方面，勞動參與率升至 61.8%，就業人口比為
59.2%，均較上月小幅上升；長期失業人數約 194.4 萬人，占總失業人口 27.1%，
大致維持穩定。產業別方面，主要產業就業變化有限，其中醫療保健增加 1.7 萬
人、營建業增加 1.1 萬人、製造業增加 0.9 萬人，金融活動則減少 0.7 萬人，政府
部門減少 1.7 萬人。薪資方面，私人部門平均時薪升至 37.81 美元，月增 0.1%、
年增 3.0%，平均每週工時維持 34.4 小時。另 7 月與 8 月新增就業合計下修 6 萬
人，反映前二月就業動能較初估疲弱。整體而言，9 月美國新增就業明顯放緩、
失業率小幅上升且薪資增速降溫，企業聘僱動能較先前轉弱；惟失業率仍維持於
近期區間，勞動市場尚未出現明顯惡化跡象，後續仍須關注企業聘僱需求是否持
續降溫，以及對聯準會利率決策的影響。 
2026 年 8 月美國職位空缺數(Job Openings) 由上月修正後之 733.5 萬個減少
25.6 萬個至 707.9 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.4%降至 4.3%， 顯示企業用人需求略有降溫 ；

惟聘僱人數由 514.6 萬人小幅增加至 519.2 萬人，聘僱率由 3.2%升至 3.3%。總離
職人數由 512.8 萬人減少至 507.0 萬人，離職率維持於 3.2%。就離職結構觀察，
主動離職(Quits)人數由 308.9 萬人降至 306.6 萬人，主動離職率維持 1.9%；裁員
與解僱(Layoffs and Discharges) 人數則由 170.2 萬人降至 164.1 萬人，裁員率由
1.1%降至 1.0%。產業別方面，職位空缺增加主要來自零售業、金融保險業、資訊
業及休閒餐飲業 ； 聘僱人數則以政府部門及製造業增幅較為明顯 。 整體而言 ，8 月
美國職缺數較前月下滑，反映企業徵才需求略有降溫，但聘僱活動仍維持穩定，
且主動離職與裁員規模同步下降，顯示勞工轉職意願及企業人力調整趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需仍大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 9 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI) 為 54.5，
較上月之 54.6 微降 0.1 點，惟已連續第九個月維持於 50 以上擴張區間，顯示製
造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由53.7 升至 55.3，需求動能續增；生
產指數由 58.3 降至 56.7，雖仍維持擴張，但成長速度略有放緩；就業指數由51.2
升至 52.7，反映企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由 59.3 微降至 59.0，交
貨延遲情況仍存；庫存指數則由 50.6 降至 48.6，企業備貨態度轉趨保守。此外，
價格指數由 71.1 升至 77.9，原物料及投入成本壓力升高；未完成訂單指數由51.8
升至 56.4，訂單去化變慢。另一方面，新出口訂單指數由53.2 降至 50.9，進口指
數亦由 52.5 降至 51.0，外需成長動能及企業備料需求略為放緩。整體而言，9 月
美國製造業活動持續穩健擴張，新訂單、就業及未完成訂單同步改善，顯示製造
業景氣仍具韌性；惟出口及進口增速放緩、投入成本壓力升高，加上關稅政策、
地緣政治風險及供應鏈不確定性仍存，後續發展仍值得持續關注。 
2026 年 8 月美國個人消費支出(PCE)物價指數月增率由上月之 0.1%升至 0.3%，
年增率維持於 3.4%； 扣除食品及能源後之核心PCE 月增率由 0.1%升至 0.2%，年
增率亦持平於 3.0%，顯示整體通膨大致持穩，惟短期物價漲勢較前月略有回升。
另一方面，個人所得月增 0.2%，可支配所得(DPI)月增 0.3%，實質可支配所得持
平；個人消費支出月增 0.9%，實質個人消費支出成長 0.6%，顯示民間消費表現
明顯回溫。由於消費增幅高於所得增幅，個人儲蓄率由前月之 4.6%降至 4.1%。
整體而言，8 月美國通膨表現大致持穩，但消費支出顯著回升，反映家庭需求仍
具韌性；後續仍須持續關注消費熱度、通膨走勢及其對聯準會貨幣政策路徑之影
響。

近期聯準會官員談話呈現鷹鴿分歧。聯準會理事 Michael Barr 認為，中東局
勢推升能源價格，以及 AI 相關投資熱潮帶動需求增長，均可能阻礙通膨回落至
2%目標 ， 未來仍不排除進一步升息的必要性 ； 惟 紐約聯準銀行總裁 John Williams、
聯準會副主席 Philip Jefferson 及金融監理副主席 Michelle Bowman 則強調，在 9
月升息後，當前並無急迫性立即進一步收緊政策，應先觀察後續通膨、就業及金
融市場數據變化，再評估是否有進一步調整利率的必要。受官員談話轉趨審慎，
以及 9 月非農就業表現疲弱影響，市場對 10 月 FOMC 會議再度升息的預期明顯
降溫，政策焦點逐漸轉向 12 月會議及年底前是否仍有一次升息空間。 
晶圓代工龍頭台積電傳出正評估與馬斯克旗下 Terafab 合作，在美國德州布
局晶圓廠，馬斯克也於社群平台 X 證實雙方正在討論，並表示 「目前僅在討論階
段 ， 但可能會有進展」 。 目前浮上檯面的合作模式包括由台積電持有並營運新廠 ，
SpaceX 或 Terafab 透過入股及承諾採購晶圓參與；另一方案則由 Terafab 掌握多
數股權 ， 台積電提供製程技術與晶圓廠營運經驗 。Terafab 旨在因應 Tesla、SpaceX
及 xAI 快速成長的 AI 晶片需求，首期投資規模達 168 億美元。若合作成真，不
僅可望成為台積電繼亞利桑那州後在美國的新布局，也凸顯先進製程與穩定晶片
供應的重要性，但目前具體合作方式、時程與投資內容仍未正式定案。 
二、歐元區—法國殖利率大幅上升，不利於投資及消費表現 
 歐元區 9 月 HICP 通膨 、 核心通膨(不含食品及能源)分別上升 0.6、0.1 個百分
點至 3.8%、2.5%， 主因國際原油價格上漲 ， 使能源通膨上升4.5 個百分點至 18.8%，
並透過肥料、物流成本上漲，間接推升食品通膨 0.3 個百分點至 1.4%。另外，服
務通膨上升 0.2 個百分點至 3.2%，近三個月服務通膨大致呈現橫向波動，部分反
映旅遊旺季因素影響。基於布蘭特原油持續位於每桶 90 美元以上，預期年底前
仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物流等成本管
道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
 其他經濟數據包含 ：(1)歐元區 8 月失業率持平 6.4%， 失業人數增加2.6 萬人 ，
主要是義大利勞動參與人數增加所致，不過 ，基於PMI 顯示近期德國勞動雇用需
求持續增溫，將有助於穩定勞動市場表現；(2)歐元區第二季家庭儲蓄率下降 0.2
個百分點至 14.2%，顯示通膨壓力削弱民眾購買力，致儲蓄暫時性減少；(3)歐元
區第二季房價年增率為 4%， 較第一季降0.6 個百分點 ， 主因法國中古屋面臨嚴格

 近一週法國 10 年期公債殖利率升至 4.9%以上，與德國公債殖利率差距擴大
至 125 個基點以上，主要反映市場對法國主權公債可持續性疑慮升溫。儘管目前
政府計畫將 2027 年財政赤字縮限至 5%，惟政府在國會並無穩定多數席次，即使
能以行政命令強行通過預算 ， 也可能面臨倒閣風險 ；此外 ，市場對於2027 年 4 月
總統大選後，新總統能否取得國會足夠支持，推動削減赤字與控制債務的改革亦
缺乏信心。因此未來法國仍可能面臨殖利率進一步上升與財政壓力加劇的風險，
暫時不利於投資及消費信心。 
三、日本—景氣溫和復甦，但消費放緩與通膨升溫風險升高 
 8 月工業生產月減 1.7%，主要係受到該月地震及豪雨等天然災害影響，導致
部分工廠暫時停產。從細項來看，汽車業受停工影響最為明顯，產量下滑6.8%，
電子產品產量亦減少 1.9%。 惟生產機械產量連續三個月成長 ， 加上核心資本財出
貨持續增加，顯示企業投資需求仍相當強勁，有助於支撐後續工業生產回升。從
企業預期來看，製造商預期9 月產量將成長 3.2%，顯示生產活動可望迅速回溫，
在供應鏈逐步恢復、庫存回補需求持續，以及穩健的企業資本支出支撐下，預計
第三季工業生產仍可維持正成長，且成長動能可望延續至年底。 
 8 月零售月減 1.2%，雖然表現略優於市場預期，但仍顯示民間消費動能正在
放緩。此次疲弱主要原因來自耐久財銷售下滑，其中家電銷售因空調等產品在換
機需求告一段落後明顯回落，汽車銷售也隨政府刺激措施效應逐漸消退而連續第
四個月下降。相較之下，食品、服裝等非耐久財銷售雖同步走弱，但降幅相對有
限 ， 反映薪資成長仍對消費提供部分支撐 。惟不利天候與通膨壓力仍是主要風險 ，
8 月至 9 月持續豪雨已影響服裝等非必需消費，而除米價以外的食品及商品價格
持續上漲，也正侵蝕家庭實質購買力，恐限制消費者信心與消費復甦力道。 
 9 月東京核心通膨年增率升至 2.7%，雖然東京都免費托兒及免費水費政策造
成的基期效應貢獻約 0.5 個百分點，但通膨升溫主因仍是價格上漲已由能源與食
品擴散至整體經濟。受石油價格上揚帶動，企業開始加速將原物料及薪資成本轉
嫁給消費者，帶動商品與服務價格普遍走高；租金通膨更升至 2%，創 1994 年以
來新高，公共交通及醫療等項目也開始調漲。隨著東京的物價壓力逐步反映至全
國數據，日本央行進一步升息的可能性升高。

四、中國—為刺激內需、引導產業走向，人行漸近式放寬政策工具 
9 月官方製造業 PMI 上升 0.3 點至 50.1，為近三個月高點，新訂單、出口訂
單指數分別為 50.5、50，並帶動生產指數上升至 51.7，不過，庫存指數下降、就
業指數加速衰退、企業展望減弱，皆拖累 PMI 指數水準。此外，投入、產出物價
指數分別上升至 60.8、54，顯示成本壓力加劇，但售價調整速度依然較為遲緩，
不利於獲利表現。官方非製造業 PMI 上升 1.2 點至 50.2，其中建築業活動指數大
增 3.4 點至 50.3，脫離連續八個月的衰退區間，主要是政府加大財政刺激力度，
推動重大建設開工；非製造業的從業人員指數為 45.9，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，
亦較不利於內需表現。 
鑒於內需動能疲弱，人行於 9 月 29 日宣布下調抵押補充貸款(該貸款本質是
將資金引導至政府希望發展的領域)利率自 1.75%降至 1.50%，並擴大適用範圍，
將水網、新型電網、算力網、新一代通信網、城市地下管網及物流網等建設納入
支持，有助於提振內需動能。未來若內需復甦力道不如預期，不排除人行將進一
步降準或擴大結構性貨幣工具支持經濟；至於全面降息則仍須兼顧人民幣匯率及
中美利差等因素，可能性相對較低。 
五、印度及東南亞—泰國、菲律賓出口均達 2 成以上的高速成長 
(1)新加坡：受服務業、零售暨其他商品業，以及食品價格漲幅擴大影響，8 月消
費者物價指數(CPI)年增率升至 2.3%、不包括住宿及個人交通費用之核心 CPI 年
增率升至 2.2%， 連續3 個月上升並創 2 年新高 。 受全球能源價格居高不下及氣候

「略微」緊縮貨幣政策。(2)泰國：8 月出口額年增 24.3%(346 億美元)，主要由工

亿美元的 GPU 贷款中扮演了关键⻆⾊。据知情⼈⼠透露，花旗、摩根⼤通、巴克莱、德意志银

国际⼤⾏打头阵，亚洲银⾏跟进
亚洲各地正在建设数百座数据中⼼，建设⽅同时在为采购芯⽚筹资。美国的 GPU 融资已增⻓迅

速，亚洲此前为数不多的此类贷款则多由私募信贷完成。
花旗担任 Zankore 在印尼 31 亿美元借款的唯⼀债务顾问，摩根⼤通担任 PaleBlueDot AI 2.55 亿美
元信贷融资的配售代理。亚洲约六位银⾏家和财务顾问表⽰，他们正在洽谈或知悉更多 GPU 相
关融资。另据知情⼈⼠上⽉透露， GMI Cloud 正与银⾏和私⼈贷款机构洽谈⼀笔 3 亿美元新贷
款，为泰国数据中⼼采购芯⽚。
据知情⼈⼠透露，在已涉⾜这⼀领域的传统贷款机构中，全球性投⾏凭借处理复杂结构的经

的⽅式 " 。 " 我们很清楚⾃⼰要的规模。我们想⾛难⾛的路，与银⾏和银团合作。 "

Document 127: 8月规模以上工业利润数据解读：8月规模以上工业利润总额同比明显回落

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月规模以上工业利润数据解读：8月规模以上工业利润总额同比明显回落.pdf
Report source: 国新证券股份
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

市场研究部 2026年9月28日
事件
9月 28日，国家统计局发布，1-8月，全国规模以上工业企业
实现利润总额 52719.8 亿元，同比增长 15.7%（按可比口径计
算）。
解读
一、1-8 月工业利润同比保持较高增长，8 月增速比 7 月明显
回落
1-8月，全国规模以上工业企业实现利润总额 52719.8 亿元，
同比增长 15.7%，比 1-7 月回落 1.9 个百分点，比 2025 年同期提
高 14.8个百分点，创 2022年以来同期同比增速新高。
其中，8月，规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长 4.2%，比 7
月回落 7.0个百分点，连续 4个月回落，比 2025 年 8月回落 16.2
个百分点。
1-8 月，采矿业利润总额同比增长 35.1%，比 1-7 月提高 0.2
个百分点，制造业利润总额同比增长 17.4%，比 1-7月回落 1.4个
百分点，电力热力水的生产供应业同比-12.0%，降幅比 1-7月扩大
6.2个百分点。
8月，规模以上工业利润总额同比增速明显回落，主要受基数

大涨的。2025 年 8 月，全国规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长
20.4%，比 7 月提高 21.9 个百分点，转降为增，当月利润改善明
显。
二、1-8 月股份制企业利润较高增长，行业利润增速极端分化
1-8月，国有控股规模以上工业企业利润总额同比增长 10.3%，
比 1-7月回落 6.0个百分点；股份制企业利润总额同比增长 20.4%，
比 1-7月回落 3.2百分点；外商及港澳台商投资企业利润总额同比
增长 2.3%，比 1-7 月提高 1.1 个百分点；私营企业利润总额增长
10.4%，比 1-7月回落 0.5个百分点。
1-8 月，在 40 个可比工业大类行业中，21 个行业利润同比增
长，19个行业利润总额同比下降。
在利润总额超千亿的行业中，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制
造业利润同比增长 109.9%，有色金属冶炼和压延加工业利润同比
宏观研究

增长 82.9%，有色金属矿采选业利润总额同比增长 71.8%，化学原

三、重点产业链利润增速较高
电子相关行业利润高速增长。1-8 月，电子行业利润同比增长
1.1 倍，与 1-7 月持平。其中，光电子器件制造、半导体分立器件
制造行业利润分别增长 72.0%、51.8%，电子专用材料制造、电子
电路制造行业利润分别增长 2.3倍、49.1%。
高技术制造业引领作用明显。1-8 月，规模以上高技术制造业
利润同比增长 54.7%，比 1-7 月提高 4.6个百分点。其中，光纤制
造、光缆制造行业利润分别增长 5.3倍和 1.0 倍；高性能服务器、
工作站、光电子通信领域中，计算机整机制造、计算机外围设备制

造、工业控制计算机及系统制造、通信系统设备制造行业利润分别
增长 3.9倍、2.6倍、1.3倍、48.7%；仪器仪表及新型医疗仪器设
备制造业加速发展，导航测绘气象及海洋专用仪器制造、口腔科用
设备及器具制造行业利润分别增长 2.2倍、70.2%。
原材料制造业利润快速增长。1-8 月，规模以上原材料制造业
利润同比增长 47.3%，比 1-7 月回落 7.9个百分点。其中，国际原
油和有色金属价格上行，带动相关行业效益改善。化工行业利润增
长 51.0%，比 1-7月回落 5.6个百分点；有色行业利润增长 82.9%，
比 1-7月回落 8.9个百分点；石油加工行业同比扭亏为盈，实现利
润总额 562.9亿元，比 1-7月增加 140.8亿元。
总体看，1-8月原材料制造业、高技术制造业、电子行业等行
业利润大幅增长，规模以上工业企业利润较高增长。8月由于基数
影响，增速明显回落。预测三季度工业利润同比增速将比二季度大
幅回落。
投资建议
1：1-8月规上原材料制造业利润总额同比较高增长。

2：1-8月规上新高技术制造业利润高增长。
3：1-8月规上电子相关行业利润高增长。
风险提示
1、地缘政治恶化超预期；2、欧美经济衰退超预期。

Document 132: 9月PMI数据解读：季末回暖，政策续力

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月PMI数据解读：季末回暖，政策续力.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
9 月份，制造业 PMI为 50.1%，环比上行 0.3 个百分点；非制造业 PMI为 50.2%，环比上行 1.2个百分点。
评论：
图1：中国 PMI主要数据一览
资料来源：Wind，中采咨询，国信证券经济研究所整理
结论：9 月制造业 PMI上行 0.3个百分点至 50.1，时隔两个月重回荣枯线上，回升幅度略弱于季节性。主
要是生产发力，需求端小幅回落但仍维持在荣枯线上。价格端大幅上行，购进价格上行 4.2 个百分点至
60.8，出厂价格上行 3.6 个百分点至 54.0，预计 PPI 增速将继续抬升。非制造业 PMI 上行 1.2 个百分点
至 50.2，重回荣枯线上。其中建筑业上行 3.4个百分点至 50.3，时隔 8 个月重回扩张区间。整体来看，9
月经济如期回暖，“金九”旺季效应有一定显现。
近期国常会提到的“推出一批务实管用的增量政策”开始逐步落地，后续四季度经济将继续回暖。9月 29
日，财政部、央行、金融监管总局联合发布居民购房贷款贴息政策，央行同日下调 PSL 利率 0.25 个百分

点至 1.5%并将水网、算力网等“六张网”建设纳入支持范围。房贷贴息的看点有二：一是中央财政首次对
商业性个人住房贷款贴息，有较强的信号意义；二是力度较为克制，贴息范围限定新增首套、120 平方米
与 150万元以内、单户上限 100万元，首年财政成本或不到百亿，后续不排除进一步加码的可能。展望四
季度，增量政策密集落地对预期的提振逐渐显现；叠加政策性金融工具加快投放与专项债使用提速，基建

### Page 2

有望边际改善。预计三季度 GDP增速回升到 4.5%，四季度继续回升至 4.7%或以上。
 制造业
9 月制造业景气度重回荣枯线上。9 月制造业 PMI上行 0.3个百分点至 50.1，同比去年高 0.3个百分点，
比近三年同期均值高 0.2个百分点。从环比变化来看，本月回升幅度略小于近三年的季节性回升（环比均
值约 0.5个百分点）。分贡献度看，9 月 PMI的回升几乎全部由生产贡献。按贡献大小分别是生产（贡献
0.325 个百分点）、原料库存（贡献 0.01个百分点）、配送（贡献 0 个百分点）、新订单（贡献-0.03 个
百分点）、雇员（贡献-0.06 个百分点）。
生产大幅回升，需求小幅回落。从相对变化看，“产需差”由负转正：生产回升（+1.3个百分点）、新订
单小幅回落（-0.1个百分点），“产需差”走阔 1.4 个百分点至 1.2个百分点，回到供强需弱的情况。从
绝对水平看，生产上行 1.3个百分点至 51.7，创 2025年 12月以来新高。“三大需求”集体下滑：新订单
（-0.1个百分点至 50.5）仍在荣枯线上，新出口订单（-0.1个百分点至 50.0）恰好位于荣枯线，在手订

单（-0.7个百分点至 46.0）仍在收缩区间。
库存分化，价格大幅上行。库存方面，原材料库存上行 0.1个百分点至 48.2，结束连续四个月下行；产成
品库存下行 0.8个百分点至 47.6，连续两个月下行。与此同时，购进价和产出价均大幅上行。购进价格上
行 4.2个百分点至 60.8，创 2026年 4月以来新高；出厂价格上行 3.6个百分点至 54.0，同样创 2026年 4
月以来新高。价格端上行信号强化，9 月 PPI环比或明显回升。
企业预期持平，小型企业景气度明显回升。制造业生产经营活动预期持平于 53.8，仍在荣枯线上，企业中
长期信心较强。分规模看，大型企业景气度持平于 50.6，连续两个月位于扩张区间；中型企业景气度上行
0.3 个百分点至 49.7，小型企业景气度上行 1.0 个百分点至 48.9，“大小企业差”收窄 1.0 个百分点至
1.7个百分点。17个制造业行业中有 9 个行业 PMI位于荣枯线上，其中专用设备、汽车制造业、农副食品
等中下游行业表现较好，装备制造业和高技术制造业 PMI 分别为 51.0%和 52.5%。

 非制造业
9 月非制造业 PMI上行 1.2个百分点至 50.2，重回荣枯线上，仍弱于近年同期（比近三年同期均值低 0.4
个百分点），但环比表现明显好于近三年同期（均值约 0 个百分点）。结构上，非制造业新订单上行 2.4
个百分点至 46.5，仍处低位；就业人员上行 0.5 个百分点至 45.9，就业景气度仍然偏弱；预期上行 0.5
个百分点至 55.5，仍在荣枯线上。
非制造业价格指标显著抬升。当月价格指标中，购进价格上行 1.4 个百分点至 52.5，出厂价格上行 1.0
个百分点至 50.3、重回荣枯线上，产出与投入的差值下行 0.4 个百分点至-2.2 个百分点，非制造业企业
利润率压力仍然偏大。
行业上，服务业与建筑业双双重回线上。服务业 PMI上行 0.9个百分点至 50.2，时隔两个月重回荣枯线上。
其中，电信广播电视及卫星传输服务、货币金融服务、保险等行业商务活动指数均位于 55.0%以上较高景
气区间；资本市场服务、房地产等行业商务活动指数均低于临界点。具体来看，居民服务下行 2.6 个百分

点到 45-50 之间，交运上行 1.5个百分点到 50-55之间，信息服务下行 0.6 个百分点到 50-55 之间。建筑
业 PMI上行 3.4个百分点至 50.3，时隔 8 个月重回扩张区间，或主要与前期极端天气影响消退、施工进度
加快有关。其中，房建中类上行 1.0个百分点，仍位于 45-50 之间。

第 1 页 共 6 页
9 月制造业 PMI 时隔两个月重回扩张区间至 50.1%
核心观点：
9 月中国制造业 PMI 为 50.1%，较上月回升 0.3 个百分点。其中，大型企业为 50.6%，
与上月持平仍在扩张区间；中型企业为 49.7%，较上月回升 0.3 个百分点；小型
企业为 48.9%，较上月回升 1 个百分点；不同规模企业的景气度仍呈分化格局。
9 月生产指数为 51.7%，较上月回升 1.3 个百分点。从需求上看，新订单指数为
50.5%，较上月回落 0.1 个百分点；而新出口订单为 50%，也较上月回落 0.1 个百
分点；9 月进口指数为 49.2%，较上月回升 0.6 个百分点。可见，9 月制造业 PMI
仍是供强需弱，生产回升幅度较大，而需求略有回落。
9 月产成品库存指数为 47.6%，较上月回落 0.8 个百分点；原材料库存指数为
48.2%，较上月回升 0.1 个百分点；9 月库存销售比（产成品库存/在手订单）为

1.03，而 8 月为 1.04。从价格指数上看，9 月出厂价格指数为 54%，较上月回升
3.6 个百分点；主要原材料购进价格为 60.8%，较上月回升 4.2 个百分点。可见，
产成品库存下滑而价格回升，显示供需关系改善。
9 月供需关系指数（新订单-产成品库存）为 2.9%，较上月回升 0.7 个百分点。9
月在手订单为 46%，较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，显示有效需求仍不足。9 月利润指
数（出厂价格-主要原材料购进价格）为-6.8%，较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，说明价
格传导仍不顺畅，制造业利润有所回落。
9 月采购量为 51%，较上月回升 0.5 个百分点；企业生产积极性指数（采购量-在
手订单）9 月为 5%，较上月回升 1.2 个百分点；制造业企业对经营前景的预期，9
月为 53.8%，与上月持平；显示制造业对经营前景的预期仍积极，企业生产积极
性已有所上升。
9 月非制造业 PMI 指数为 50.2%，较上月回升 1.2 个百分点，也重回扩张区间。其
中，服务业为 50.2%，较上月回升 0.9 个百分点；而建筑业为 50.3%，较上月回升

3.4 个百分点，创出年内新高。9 月综合 PMI 产出指数为 50.7%，较 8 月回升 1.2
百分点，说明我国企业生产经营活动总体较上月有所扩张。
风险提示：
对上述事件发展趋势的点评，存在由于经济增长、行业竞争、外部经济环境和政
策等变化，而不如预期的可能。

一、9 月制造业 PMI 回升至扩张区间还是靠生产拉动
9 月 30 日国家统计局公布 9 月中国制造业 PMI 为 50.1%，较 8 月回升 0.3 个百分点，时隔
两个月重新回到 50%的荣枯线之上（图 1 所示）。其中，9 月生产指数为 51.7%，较 8 月回升
1.3 个百分点，连续两个月处于扩张区间。
图 1 2026 年 8-9 月中国制造业 PMI 构成指数面积雷达图

宏观经济

第 3 页 共 6 页
从需求上（图 2 所示）看，9 月新订单为 50.5%，较上月回落 0.1 百分点；而 9 月新出
口订单为 50%，同样较上月回落 0.1 个百分点。而从在一定程度上代表内需的进口指数看，9
月为 49.2%，较上月回升 0.6 个百分点，仍处于收缩区间。可见，9 月制造业仍呈现供强需弱的
格局，生产回升幅度更大，需求变化不大。
另外，9 月非制造业 PMI 指数为 50.2%（图 3 所示），较上月回升 1.2 个百分点，
时 隔两 个 月再 次 回到 扩张 区 间。 其 中， 服 务业 为 50.2%， 较上 月 回升 0.9 个 百分 点 ；而
建筑业为 50.3%，较上月大幅回升 3.4 个百分点，创出今年新高。
图 3 9 月非制造业 PMI 指数为 50.2%时隔两月再次回到扩张区间
资料来源：国家统计局，第一创业证券研究所整理。
9 月综合 PMI 产出指数为 50.7%，是今年以来的新高，较 8 月回升 1.2 百分点，时隔两个月

再次回到扩张区间，表明我国企业生产经营活动总体较上月扩张。
二、9 月制造业 PMI 显示供需改善使企业生产积极性有所上升
从代表供求关系的具体指数角度看，用新订单减去产成品库存来代表供需关系（图 4 显示），
9 月这一指数 2.9，较上月回升 0.7 个百分点，主要得益于产成品库存的回落；而 9 月在手订单
指数为 46%，较上月回落 0.7 个百分点，仍处于 50%以下的收缩区间，显示有效需求仍不足。
以采购量与在手订单之差来代表企业生产积极性，两者之差 9 月为 5%，较上月回升 1.2 个
百分点，处于一年来的最高水平，显示制造业企业的生产积极性有所上升。
从总体上看，制造业企业对生产经营活动的预期，9 月为 53.8%，与上月持平，仍处于 50%
以上的扩张区间，显示制造业企业对企业经营前景的预期仍持积极态度。

制造业企业利润增速将有所回落。
三、9 月制造业 PMI 中不同规模企业的景气度分化
9 月不同规模制造业企业间的景气度出现分化（图 6 所示）。其中，大型企业 9 月 PMI 指
数为 50.6%，与上月持平仍处扩张区间；中型企业 9 月 PMI 为 49.7%，较上月回升 0.3 个百分

点；小型企业 9 月 PMI 为 48.9%，较上月回升 1 个百分点，中小企业景气度处于收缩区间。
图 6 9 月制造业 PMI 指数中不同规模企业景气度分化

从就业情况看，9 月从业人员指数为 48.4%，较上月回落 0.3 个百分点，仍处于 50%以下收
缩区间，说明制造业企业用工景气度下滑，就业状况总体上仍不容乐观。
从物流情况看，9 月供应商配送时间为 50.1%，与上月持平仍处扩张区间，显示制造业原材
料供应商的交货时间继续加快。

宏观策略

2026年10月6日
全球宏观 
加息或暂缓而非结束——9月美国非农数据点评
9月美国新增非农数据偏弱，前个两月的数据也下修。美国9月新增非农
2.9万，市场预期增加9万。之前数据修正也以下修为主，8月数据第一次
修正下修2.9万人到增加13.3万人，7月数据第二次修正下修至减少1万
人，但相对于初值上修1.3万人。
季调可能也有干扰，9月非季调的新增非农仍略强于去年。非季调的非农
9月增加了33.3万人，去年9月增加30.5万人。经季调后新增非农的2.9
万人则低于去年同期修正后的7.6万人。
9月美国失业率小幅上升，但劳动参与率回升。9月美国失业率从4.1%小
幅上升到4.2%。对比去年9月的4.4%。从周期角度看，目前失业率水平仍
处于较低水平，远未达到任何历史衰退时期的水平。同时失业率的上升部
分来自于分母变化，劳动参与率由61.6%进一步上升至61.8%，本月失业
率上升并非主要由裁员推动，而是更多人口重新进入劳动力市场。
9月美国工资压力环比略有缓解，平均时薪同比增速仍下降。9月美国私
营非农企业全体雇员平均时薪环比上涨0.1%，同比涨幅为3.0%，低于8月
同比的3.1%。平均每周工时维持在34.4小时。
最新高频数据显示美国失业水平仍处于相对低位。截至9月26日当周，季
调后美国初次申领失业金人数为19.7万人，略低于上一周，近4周平均
20.0万人，仍处于相对低位。与此同时，最新公布的8月JOLTS数据显示，
招聘率略有回升，但仍距离过热水平较远，辞职率则变化不大，维持低
位。
我们预期加息周期并未结束，但节奏或有调整，美联储可能10月暂缓加
息。数据公布前，利率期货市场显示10月加息概率不到3成，12月加息
是基准情形；数据公布后，10月加息概率进一步降至仅约2成。尽管9月
美国非农数据偏弱，但是尚不足以令美联储就此结束加息周期，我们认
为，美联储大概率继续等待更多数据，尤其是通胀数据公布后再做决策。
我们维持此前预计美联储年内可能再加息一次的观点，加息时点倾向于
12月。
美债收益率先降后升，美股三大股指上行。10月2日，美国三大股指均上
涨，其中纳斯达克领涨1.19%，标普上涨0.73%，道琼斯指数上涨0.49%。
分行业看，标普500行业指数中仅医疗保健下跌，其他行业指数均上涨，
其中非必需消费、信息技术和材料板块涨幅靠前。美国国债收益率盘中一
度下行，但收盘普遍略微上行，我们认为偏弱的就业数据降低了近期加息
预期，但尚未显著改变市场对中期通胀和利率维持高位的预期。10年期
国债收益率全日上升4个基点，1年期收益率利率上升2个基点，3个月国
债收益率上升2个基点。美元指数当日跌约0.17%。

### Page 8

评级定义
分析员个股评级定义 : 分析员行业评级定义：
买入：预期个股未来12个月的总回报高于相关行业。
中性：预期个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业一致。
沽出：预期个股未来12个月的总回报低于相关行业
无评级：对于个股未来12个月的总回报与相关行业的比较，
分析员并无确信观点。
领先：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数具吸引力。
同步：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现与大盘标杆
指数一致。
落后：分析员预期所覆盖行业未来12个月的表现相对于大盘
标杆指数不具吸引力。
香港市场的标杆指数为恒生综合指数，A股市场的标杆指数
为MSCI中国A股指数，美国上市中概股的标杆指数为标普美
国中概股50（美元）指数

Document 174: August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/August CPI – Came in on our expectations (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
August CPI – Came in on our expectations
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
15:27 September 30 2026 Share
August Monthly CPI: Headline 0.4%mth/4.0%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. Market
services ex volatile –0.4%mth/3.3%yr.

The CPI rose 0.4% in August, spot on Westpac’s expectation but a bit softer than market
expectations of 0.5%. Due to base effects the annual pace picked up to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline result were auto fuels (14.8%); games, toys & hobbies (10.2%) with
rents providing a solid base (0.3%) while some offset was provided by both domestic (–4.9%) and
international holiday travel & accommodation (–1.5%).
Trimmed mean (TM) inflation printed 0.2% again bang on Westpac’s expectation while the market
was slightly higher at 0.3%. The annual pace was flat at 3.6%yr, the third month in a row at this pace,
while the six-month annualise pace was steady at 3.9%yr.

Key factors driving the TM print were: rents (0.3%) and new dwelling purchases (0.2%). The TM
distribution ranged from –0.38% to 0.82%.
Today’s inflation update revealed price discounting for some durable goods as retail demand softens.
We would expect to see a bounce from these categories in the following month. There was also
offsetting strength elsewhere in durable good so at least for now, the momentum continues to
suggest some near term upside risk to our current inflation estimates.

Nevertheless, as the TM came in on expectations and based on a first quick pass at updating the
quarterly profile, we see little pressure to revise our current Q3 estimates of 1.3%qtr for the CPI and
0.9%qtr for the TM.
The RBA remains alert to near term inflationary risks. We are expecting the momentum in TM
inflation to remain well above the top of the target band through to year-end underpinning our
expectations for the RBA to raise rates again in November.

### Page 3

August CPI in more detail
Following the bumper read in July, the monthly CPI moderated in August rising 0.4%mth. This was in
line with our expectations and slightly softer than the market consensus. This took the year-ended pace
to 4.0%yr. On a seasonally adjusted basis the CPI rose 0.7%mth highlighting the impact of seasonal
sales in August.

They key drivers behind the monthly lift were automotive fuel, up 14.8%mth, contributing 0.5ppts to the
headline figure. The surge resulted from the ending of the fuel excise discount on August 3 and rising
global energy prices. With crude oil prices still elevated due to the conflict in the middle east, and
growing shortages of refined products due to both the middle east conflict and Ukrainian assaults on
Russian refining capacity, auto fuel is expected to remain an inflationary impulse at least until the end
of 2026.

The monthly TM also softened, rising 0.2%mth compared to the 0.5%mth read from July. The result
was again in line with our expectations, and very close at two decimal places (0.24%mth vs
0.23%mth), but softer than the market's view. The year-ended pace held at 3.6%yr for the third
consecutive month, although it continued to ease marginally at the second decimal place. In contrast,
the six-month annualised pace has continued to trend higher since April and is currently running at
3.9%yr.

### Page 4

There is a possibility that as the ABS continues to grapple with seasonal adjustment issues we will see
revisions to the TM estimates. Last July also saw a strong 0.48%mth read which eased to 0.29%mth in
August. This is not too dissimilar from the 0.48%mth and 0.24%mth reads for 2026.

We are expecting core inflation to start to moderate as we get to year end but our current estimate for
the month of December still has the TM running at a 3.3% in the year while the six-month annualised
pace is still a chunky 3.1%. Applying a trim on the seasonally adjusted 3m/3m expenditure classes
yields a TM of 0.9%qtr currently. This is in line with our current Q3 TM estimate.

Other measures of underlying inflation told a similar story, easing on a monthly basis but holding steady
on a year-ended basis. Market services ex volatiles fell –0.4%mth to be 3.3%yr higher. Excluding both
volatiles & holidays saw a 0.2%mth/4.1%yr lift.

Most market services components eased in the month (including on a seasonally adjusted basis) but
annual growth across this group as a whole remained above the RBA's 2-3% target band. The near-
term momentum (three-month annualised) is also quite strong across several components of services.
Of note include deposit and loan facilities (11.2%mth), sports participation (10.9%), clothing repair
(7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other household services (5.2%). We are closely watching market
services as this is where inflationary risks, outside of energy, appear to be most persistent.

Due to its sizable weight in the CPI, and the fact price changes here tend to be less volatile than many
other categories, so they almost always appear in the TM estimates, housing prices remain a key
focus. Rents rose 0.3%mth in August, in line with our expectations, to be up 3.6%yr. Since April, the
seasonally adjusted rents series has been running lower than the non-seasonally adjusted series. We
are expecting rents to hold around this pace till year end before moderating modestly as we move
through 2027.

New dwellings inflation was 0.2%mth in August (softer than our expectation for a 0.4%mth rise). Since
peaking in May, monthly price growth has slowed in each successive month, potentially reflecting
increasing difficulty for developers in passing through higher costs amid softer conditions in established
housing markets and weaker demand in the face of higher interest rates. The annual pace looks to
have topped out. It was at 5.8%yr in June easing to 5.4%yr currently with the three-month annualised
pace at 3.7%. We expect dwelling price inflation to continue easing through to the end of 2027 as
falling established house prices limit the ability of builders to pass on rising construction costs. There is
a medium-term risk that as conditions improve builders may raise prices more aggressively to claw
back margins.

### Page 5

Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated in August. The share of the basket running above 3%yr
eased from 45% to 42%, with the share below 2%yr increasing from 38% to 41%.

### Page 6

Key surprises in the month:
Upside surprises

Downside surprises
Insurance surprised to the upside (0.7%mth vs 0.3%mth) after a run of weaker-than-usual reads over
the past two months.
Motor vehicle repair costs rose a solid 0.8% against our 0.3%mth expectations, with spare parts and
accessories also up 0.3%mth vs –0.4%mth.
Footwear rose 1.7%mth against our expectations for a -2.9%mth decline, the gains were broad-
based across women, men & children.
Household goods surprised to the upside. Household appliances, utensils and tools rose 1.9%mth
against our flat expectations, meanwhile household textiles fell –1.0%mth against our expectations
for a –5.0%mth fall. Furniture and furnishings also fell by less than expected –0.6%mth vs –1.3%mth.
Recreation and culture surprised to the upside (–1.0%mth vs –1.5%mth), owing to a 1.4%mth lift in
other recreation, sport & culture against our expectations for a flat read on the back of an outsized
gain in games, toys & hobbies (10.2%mth vs 2.5%mth). Holiday travel was marginally less weak than
expected (–3.3% vs –3.5%mth).

### Page 7

Food was the key downside miss falling –0.1%mth against our expectations for a 0.2%mth lift. Meat
& seafood (–0.9%mth vs 0.5%mth) and fruit & vegetables (–1.2%mth vs 0.1%mth) were the key
areas of weakness.
Audio, visual and computing equipment and services came in weaker-than-expected (0.7%mth vs
1.2%mth), owing to a softer 0.6%mth lift in computing services (3.0%mth expected). This was partly
balanced by a stronger than expected lift in computing equipment (0.9%mth vs –0.5%mth). The latter

is where we are most likely to see the impact of the AI boom in the CPI, due to computer chip
inflation, other than construction cost inflation for new dwellings.
Motor vehicles came in lower at –0.4%mth vs –0.1%mth which could be the start of price pressures
associated with the rapid expansion of the share of Chinese cars in the Australian market.
Automotive fuel was also slightly softer at 14.8%mth vs 16.7%mth.
Garments and accessories were both softer. Garments fell –1.9%mth against our expectations for a

0.2%mth rise, meanwhile accessories came in at 0.1%mth vs 0.5%mth expected.

### Page 8

What does this mean for the RBA?
Yesterday, post the RBA rate hike and monetary policy decision statement, Westpac changed its view
from a November follow up rate hike being a risk to being the base case. The post-meeting statement
was notable for its emphasis on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices are now
much higher than had been assumed in the August forecasts”. In addition, RBA’s liaison suggests firms
are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-through was characterised as partial, and additional

to domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We read this as implying that the RBA
is concerned about upside risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.

### Page 9

Related articles
Our current forecast of 0.9%qtr for the September quarter TM stands post today’s release and
represents a step up from 0.8% in the June quarter holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. Our current
forecast for the Q4 TM is 0.7%qtr/3.3%yr but given the move in energy prices, and anecdotes of firms
now passing on those rising costs, we too see upside risks to this estimate.

As such, we hold to the view that the RBA will follow up with another rate hike in November. For more
information on the RBA decision please see Luci Ellis’s note “RBA hikes in September, November
follow-up now expected".

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy

ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều

tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

### Page 3

3

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng

được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

Joseph Quinlan

MACRO STRATEGY 
MARKET VIEW 
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK 
MARKETS IN REVIEW 
Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
I
N THIS  ISSUE
Macro Strategy—The  Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran: Bessent’s Three-Front War and
the Stakes for
Investors: Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury secretary, but
these days he looks more like America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches

from T
okyo, where he is trying to engineer a stronger Japan yen, to the U.S. Treasury
market, where he is battling to keep the 10-year yield from breaking  decisively above
5%, and to T
ehran, where he has become the point man in the escalating U.S. campaign
to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Ho
w Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A decisive victory on
all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets is next to impossible,

while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best laid plans. That said, however,
the U.S. Secretary of the Treasury has signaled that he is ready to take a very  proactive,
pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less encouraging news stems
from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the Treasury secretary.
Market View—Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”: Behind
ever
y Artificial Intelligence (AI)-generated response is a vast physical network of data

centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems, semiconductor facilities
and critical-mineral supply chains. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power,
water, advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and
intensify pressure
on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend
beyon
d AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

investment in power generation,

advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.

investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

 ; 
◼ 
 Risk profile: 
 strong economic growth and low public 
 and  
foreign debt vs. low external liquidity and 
 a 
weak 
banking sector.

Vietnam’s economic success story has continued

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

Document 229: Five reasons why French growth should pick up (2026-09-29)

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.35 / 28 September 2026EDITORIAL
FIVE REASONS WHY FRENCH GROWTH SHOULD PICK UP
1 We will review this in an EcoFlash scheduled for publication on 1st October.
2 The municipal election cycle (around which local investment always contracts)
has weighed on the economy, as well as the decline in agricultural production (more
heavily than elsewhere in the Eurozone, notably due to the weight of corn production

in France).
3 This has led to additional expenditure: debt servicing (indexed bonds made
more expensive by rebounding inflation) and other spending (fuel subsidies and
the budget for the military's external operations).
In 2026, the government once again failed to stabilise its public debt-to-GDP ratio due to a significant fiscal deficit and
lower-than-usual growth. According to our analysis, this underperformance is due to exceptional factors. Structurally,

France can still generate growth that will help the public debt ratio to stabilise, provided that its budget deficit is brought
down to approximately 3% of GDP . To achieve this, it has significant strengths: 1/ Improved competitiveness in terms of la-
bour and energy costs; 2/ Its services sector, notably through the development and use of new technologies (including AI);
3/ An industrial recovery driven by aeronautics, military spending and electrification, which calls for increased invest-

ment; 4/ Accelerating business creation that supports employment; 5/ Its attractiveness to foreign investors. All of these
structural drivers should enable French growth to rebound and join the expected acceleration in the Eurozone in 2027.
The French government will present its 2027 draft budget 1, while
the presidential election looms on the horizon. Ahead of these major
events, the French people are appearing very pessimistic about the
picture in France. The latest survey by the Jean Jaurès Foundation re -

veals that nearly two out of three people use only negative words, with
"worry" and "uncertainty" at the forefront. Furthermore, three-quar -
ters expect the country's situation to deteriorate.
Indeed, the French economy has faced a series of setbacks since the
beginning of the year. It narrowly avoided recession in the first half
(a 0.2% q/q contraction of GDP in Q1 and stagnation in Q2), in contrast
to the Eurozone, where growth excluding Ireland remained stable

(0.3% q/q per quarter).
Even though exceptional factors are behind this underperformance 2,
it is weighing on public finances (lower revenues), just like another
exceptional factor: the conflict in Iran 3. Consequently, the fiscal deficit
is expected to increase to 5.4% of GDP in 2026 (compared to 5.1% in
2025), according to the government. According to our forecasts, the pu-
blic debt-to-GDP ratio would reach 119% in 2026, compared to 115.7%
in 2025.

Against this backdrop, France's ability to generate growth is crucial.
Yet, even with fiscal consolidation which should, by its very nature,
weigh on demand, we can identify five reasons to believe that the
French economy can generate growth significantly above 2026 outco -
me (0.5% according to our forecasts).
1. FRANCE IS MORE COMPETITIVE
During the Eurozone crisis (2012–13), France implemented a policy
to reduce labour costs through the Competitiveness and Employment

Tax Credit (CICE). This was subsequently replaced, at the beginning of
Emmanuel Macron's first term, by a 6-pp reduction in social security
contributions. These labour-cost improvements persist today, whereas
we have seen an acceleration over the last decade in wage growth
elsewhere in Europe, notably in Germany. Alongside the relative impro-
vement in French labour costs, the bilateral trade deficit with Germany
has significantly decreased.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.35 / 28 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
At the same time, France benefits from cheaper electricity which is also
less sensitive to rising hydrocarbon prices because, in 2025, nearly 95%
of electricity was produced through nuclear and renewable power 4.
Furthermore, electricity accounts for 27% of energy consumption in
France, compared to a European average of 24%.
2. FRANCE: A EUROPEAN LEADER IN SERVICES, INCLUDING TECH, AND IN

UPWARDLY REVISED GROWTH FIGURES
Services account for nearly 80% of French GDP , which is significantly
higher than in the other major Eurozone countries. France is leading
the race within the European Union in several sectors, including finan-
cial services, logistics and tech. This is resulting in a services trade
surplus of nearly 1.7% of GDP , which is offsetting an equivalent external
deficit in goods. This is also behind the investment in intellectual pro-

perty products of around 5.5% of GDP in 2025, the highest rate among
the major European countries.
Furthermore, nearly one in two French people now uses AI, compared
to less than one-third in the United States, according to a study by the
Microsoft AI Economy Institute. France ranks 4 th in the world in this
field. In Europe, it is surpassed only by Ireland (where many compa -
nies in the sector are concentrated). France's combined advantages
(energy and digital culture) place it at the forefront of destinations

for artificial-intelligence investment in Europe, particularly for data
centres (announcements were made during the most recent Choose
France summit).
Additionally, France is the country that revises its growth figures
upwards most frequently. In a recent study, we showed that ex-post re-
visions (as national accounts are only finalised 30 months after the end
of a calendar year) were significant there. This phenomenon is likely
linked to the weighting of tech in the investment of French companies,

with the impact on activity measured more easily along time.
3. THE INDUSTRIAL RECOVERY IS TEMPORARILY SLOWED BY SUPPLY
CONSTRAINTS
The French industrial rebound is real and is supported, in particular,
by strengthening French comparative advantages in aeronautics and
electrical equipment. It relies on rising global demand for these goods
and a European momentum that has been accelerating for several
quarters. As a result, French goods exports increased by 5% in the early

months of 2026 compared to the same period in 2025.
However, the industrial recovery is being hampered by the difficulty
faced by companies in meeting demand. Since the end of the COVID-19
pandemic, industrial production has experienced a bumpy recovery.
The most recent of output surges helped French growth to outperform
between the 2nd and 4th quarters of 2025, when aeronautical production
suddenly increased by nearly 20%. The increased delivery targets of

the major companies in the sector could have driven further growth.
This did not materialise, as manufacturing production decreased by
1.2% over the last three months for which data has been published
(May–July) compared to the previous three months.
Production for companies with the most filled order books (aeronau -
tics, electrical and electronic equipment) has been limited by a sharp
rise in supply constraints. The production capacity of industrial com -

panies puts them in a position to meet demand less effectively than
before, due to both shortages of skilled labour and an insufficient nu -
mber of production lines. This situation calls for more investment. The
good news is that, according to the latest quarterly survey from Insee,
investment intentions in the industry have rebounded significantly.
4 With the exception of 2022 (maintenance of nuclear power plants).
4. STRONG BUSINESS CREATIONS SUPPORT EMPLOYMENT

When France undergoes a period of recession or low growth, business
creation also tends to dive. This was the case during the Eurozone
crisis in the first half of the 2010s, but that is not what we are seeing
in 2026. Business creation has even seen its growth accelerate since
the beginning of 2026, standing at +12% y/y. It is increasing more si -
gnificantly in digital-related sectors (+46% y/y), which are benefiting
from the wave of AI-related innovation. However, it is also progressing

elsewhere, such as in trade (+13%) and construction (+9%).
Business creation is even increasing faster than business insolvencies
(+5% y/y in the first half), with the rise in insolvencies largely driven by
stronger business creations.

The bank
for a changing
world
The increase in business creation is noticeable among companies (+5%)
and even more so among micro-enterprises (+16%). This reflects the
rise in self-employment. While payroll employment has seen a slight
decline over the last two years (80,000 net job losses), part of this
slowdown is linked to the increase in self-employment (204,000 net
creations). Overall, France has continued to create jobs and the em -

ployment rate has reached historic highs.
5. FRANCE REMAINS ATTRACTIVE TO FOREIGN INVESTMENT
French has maintained its attractiveness despite the higher uncer -
tainty. Uncertainty increased significantly following the dissolution
of the National Assembly in June 2024, according to estimates from
the Banque de France and Insee. However, France remains at the top
of the European countries in international attractiveness rankings.
The annual EY barometer notes a decrease in the number of projects in

2025 (-17%), but France has maintained its first-place position for the
7th consecutive year. Furthermore, against a European backdrop where
the number of jobs per project has dropped sharply (-25%), it has re -
mained overall stable in France (-4%). This is a sign that, while there
are slightly fewer investments in number, their footprint on the French
economy remains steady.
France's attractiveness is driven in particular by the development of
services, as well as the quality of its infrastructure and logistical capa-

bilities (including transport hubs, often on a European scale). Moreo -
ver, while France already boasts a developed tech sector, the country
is well-positioned to attract foreign investment in AI (low energy costs
and skilled labour).
********
French growth should return in 2027 to a level more in line with its
strengths (our forecast is 1%). Given the prevailing interest rate condi-
tions, this is a necessary condition5 for the public debt ratio to stabilise,

provided that the government succeeds in bringing the fiscal deficit
down to 3% of GDP .
Stéphane Colliac
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
5 This is also a sufficient condition, provided that the effective interest rate remains below 3.5% (it is expected to stand at 2.1% in 2026 and 3.2% in 2030, according to
our scenario).

### Page 1

Marvell 预计 2028 财年总营收将达 200 亿美元，⾼于此前给出的 180 亿美元，也超过华尔街
预期的 182 亿美元；预计 2031 财年营收将达到 700 亿⾄ 900 亿美元，远超华尔街预期的
468.5 亿美元。消息公布后， Marvell 股价⼀度上涨近 9% 。
摘要：
Marvell Technology 在投资者⽇进⼀步上调⻓期业绩⽬标，预计 2028 财年总营收将达到约 200 亿
美元，⾼于此前给出的 180 亿美元⽬标，也超过华尔街约 182 亿美元的预期。
公司同时预计， 2031 财年营收将达到 700 亿⾄ 900 亿美元。明显⾼于华尔街⽬前约 468.5 亿美元
的预期。
这⼀上调反映出 Marvell 对 AI 基础设施需求的判断更加乐观。公司称，数据中⼼芯⽚需求持续增
⻓，尤其是定制芯⽚业务正在成为重要增⻓引擎。 AI 基础设施投⼊扩⼤，也推动市场对 Marvell
定制计算和互连芯⽚的需求。
消息公布后， Marvell 股价⼀度上涨近 9% ，随后涨幅有所收窄；竞争对⼿博通股价早盘⼀度上涨

约 4% 。 Marvell 股价今年以来已经上涨超过 3 倍。
Marvell 在 8 ⽉公布第⼆财季业绩时已经上调过⼀次⻓期预期。当时公司将 2027 财年营收预期上
调⾄约 120 亿美元，并将 2028 财年营收预期从 165 亿美元上调⾄约 180 亿美元。
2031 财年营收⽬标最⾼ 900 亿美元，远超预期
相⽐ 2028 财年的 200 亿美元⽬标， Marvell 对更⻓期的增⻓预期更加激进。
公司预计 2031 财年营收将达到 700 亿⾄ 900 亿美元，中值为 800 亿美元。以 2026 财年 82 亿美元的
营收为基数计算，意味着 Marvell 未来数年的年复合营收增速约为 55% ⾄ 60% 。
